---
name: using-superpowers
description: Use when starting any conversation - establishes how to find and use skills, requiring skill invocation before ANY response including clarifying questions
---

<SUBAGENT-STOP>
If you were dispatched as a subagent to execute a specific task, ignore this skill.
</SUBAGENT-STOP>

<EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>
If you think there is even a 1% chance a skill might apply to what you are doing, you ABSOLUTELY MUST invoke the skill.

IF A SKILL APPLIES TO YOUR TASK, YOU DO NOT HAVE A CHOICE. YOU MUST USE IT.

This is not negotiable. You cannot rationalize your way out of this.
</EXTREMELY-IMPORTANT>

## The Rule

**Invoke relevant or requested skills BEFORE any response or action** — including clarifying questions, exploring the codebase, or checking files. If it turns out wrong for the situation, you don't have to use it.

**Before entering plan mode:** if you haven't already brainstormed, invoke the brainstorming skill first.

Then announce "Using [skill] to [purpose]" and follow the skill exactly. If it has a checklist, create a todo per item.

## Skill Priority

When multiple skills apply, process skills come first — they set the approach, then implementation skills (frontend-design, etc.) carry it out. Brainstorming and systematic-debugging are Superpowers' most common process skills, but the rule holds for any of them.

- "Let's build X" → superpowers:brainstorming first, then implementation skills.
- "Fix this bug" → superpowers:systematic-debugging first, then domain skills.

## Red Flags

These thoughts mean STOP—you're rationalizing:

| Thought | Reality |
|---------|---------|
| "This is just a simple question" | Questions are tasks. Check for skills. |
| "I need more context first" | Skill check comes BEFORE clarifying questions. |
| "Let me explore the codebase first" | Skills tell you HOW to explore. Check first. |
| "I can check git/files quickly" | Files lack conversation context. Check for skills. |
| "Let me gather information first" | Skills tell you HOW to gather information. |
| "This doesn't need a formal skill" | If a skill exists, use it. |
| "I remember this skill" | Skills evolve. Read current version. |
| "This doesn't count as a task" | Action = task. Check for skills. |
| "The skill is overkill" | Simple things become complex. Use it. |
| "I'll just do this one thing first" | Check BEFORE doing anything. |
| "This feels productive" | Undisciplined action wastes time. Skills prevent this. |
| "I know what that means" | Knowing the concept ≠ using the skill. Invoke it. |

## Platform Adaptation

If your harness appears here, read its reference file for special instructions:

- Codex: `references/codex-tools.md`
- Pi: `references/pi-tools.md`
- Antigravity: `references/antigravity-tools.md`

## User Instructions

User instructions (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, GEMINI.md, etc, direct requests) take precedence over skills, which in turn override default behavior. Only skip skill workflows or instructions when your human partner has explicitly told you to.

# Moriarty footguns

Standing instructions adopted by the [2026-09-06 user reset](../raw/assignments/moriarty-target-first-reset-2026-09-06.md).
Read with [the postmortem](postmortems/2026-09-06-moriarty-verification-detour.md).
These rules apply to research, planning, implementation and recovery.

1. **Recover the purpose before the work queue.** Read the latest user directive
   and current roadmap notice before checkpoint obligations. A stale loop is
   not authority to continue. A4/A5 are unfinished historical experiments; the
   current task is the ACTUS/DeFi/PCD design cycle. Never report them complete to
   clear an obsolete goal.

2. **Start semantics with implementation targets.** Every proposed semantic
   operation must trace to an ACTUS fixture, a named DeFi behavior or an explicit
   developer requirement. Give the smallest example that requires it. Do not
   defer both target families until after selecting and verifying a Core.
   Product types and taxonomy categories do not automatically become Core
   constructors. Keep the complete target coverage matrix visible.

3. **Show what a developer can do.** Before a large formalization campaign,
   show representative authoring, simulation, failure diagnosis, signing and
   proof-consumption flows. Name what works, what is mocked and what is missing.
   A test-count checklist cannot replace this demonstration.

4. **Name the correctness claim.** Separate language metatheorems,
   contract-specific properties, a transaction's valid execution, PCD history
   compliance, and compiler/ledger correspondence. State assumptions, input
   domains and bounds. A passing model check, cryptographic proof or compiler
   invocation establishes only its actual predicate.

5. **Turing-incomplete does not mean automatically correct.** Require explicit
   sizes for values, intermediate arithmetic, collections, schedules, horizons,
   nesting, transaction work and predecessor fan-in. Specify rounding, overflow,
   rejection and termination. Finite state may still be too large to enumerate.
   Prefix checking needs a completeness argument before becoming a lifetime
   claim. Continuations must not silently reset a promised lifecycle bound.

6. **PCD is part of transaction acceptance.** The design must bind a transaction
   to its semantic/program versions, predecessors, authorization, observations,
   resulting state and effects. Specify constrained genesis, multi-input
   composition and the final verification decision. Hash-linked receipts and
   simulated certificates are not PCD. Missing or invalid required proofs must
   fail closed in the real acceptance path. A signed mandatory-claim root cannot
   be stripped or downgraded by a relay. An optional acceleration fallback must
   preserve the same required claim/history predicate.

7. **History compliance is not global uniqueness or oracle truth.** Define
   ledger consumption/nullifiers, ordering, finality and external-input trust
   separately. Test duplicate consumption and competing valid branches, as well
   as altered proof bytes and public inputs. State what a signature attests.

8. **Check backend compatibility early and narrowly.** Recursive verification,
   folding, accumulation, compression and zero knowledge are different
   properties. Compact's ban on recursive source functions does not rule out
   Midnight-native recursive proofs. Inspect the actual native implementation
   and its application/ledger interface separately. Name the construction and
   final verifier. Do not infer Nova–Compact compatibility from either project's existence. After specifying the
   required relation, use one small positive proof and meaningful rejection
   controls to test the actual pinned deployment interface.

9. **Bound investigations by a decision.** Before an expensive run, record the
   question, smallest decisive input, expected distinguishing outcomes, command,
   resource ceiling and stop condition in the task's existing record. Track
   cumulative effort against that ceiling. After a failure, change a justified
   hypothesis before repeating work. Do not automatically increase heaps,
   widen matrices, rerun consumed stages or arm an open-ended completion loop.
   Use an existing authorized resource budget; if none exists, propose a bounded
   experiment as part of planning rather than inventing unlimited authority.

10. **Keep process proportional.** Preserve authentic inputs, outputs and failed
    runs. Reuse those receipts. Add review or infrastructure only when it closes
    a named correctness or reproducibility gap; do not turn tooling repair,
    provenance, checkpointing or Foreman development into the product. A
    source-only review does not approve product suitability or a native result.

11. **Reuse evidence with its original scope.** E00 generated a narrow Compact
    atomic-swap specialization; its proof compilation was mock. Candidate A
    contains useful interpreter, authorization and lifecycle experiments, with
    incomplete A4/A5 acceptance. Neither is a finished general DSL. Do not erase
    useful code, promote it automatically, or force the new semantics to fit it.

12. **Report progress in user terms.** Lead checkpoints with the current goal,
    usable capability, material gaps and next reviewable deliverable. Include
    ACTUS, DeFi, proofs and developer interface status. Cite test counts only as
    supporting evidence. Report runner counters as counters, not a billing
    invoice or a measured total of wasted compute.

The current [design-cycle plan](superpowers/plans/2026-09-06-actus-defi-pcd-replanning.md)
applies these rules. Changing the plan requires preserving the reason and its
effect on target coverage, not adding another layer of approval machinery.

13. **Separate authority from outcomes and concrete plans.** Exact-plan signing
    is a restricted profile, not solver-independent intent. A refund cannot
    conceal gross over-spending, and a gross receipt before fees is not the net
    promised delivery. Check permitted intermediate recipients and calls.
    Pending progress carries residual authority and obligations; it does not
    establish a terminal goal. A valid signature, keyword certificate or
    taxonomy classification proves none of these semantics. See the
    [intents amendment](research/2026-09-06-intents-report-integration.md).

     1	(* Proposal only: moriarty-bounded-atomic/1. Specified-only. No parser, freeze, or audit. *)
     2	(* Source is UTF-8. Spans are 0-based UTF-8 byte offsets [start, end). *)
     3	(* Whitespace: ASCII space, tab, CR, LF between tokens. No source comments. *)
     4	(* Lexer uses longest operator match for ==, <=, >=. No !=. *)
     5	(* A word that exactly equals a keyword is a keyword, otherwise it may be an identifier. *)
     6	(* Keywords cannot be identifiers. Identifiers are 1..64 ASCII [A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*. *)
     7	(* Binary operators associate left. Comparisons cannot chain. There is no plain division. *)
     8	(* Numeric, schema, units, lifecycle, horizon, and capacity checks are semantic. *)
     9	(* Header horizon is an unsigned UTC epoch-second upper bound, not host-clock access. *)

   147	(* 1. At least one action_decl. Duplicate names reject inside each namespace. *)
   148	(* 2. Units, consts, state, observations, and effect schemas must be *)
   149	(*    declared before value use. Locals cannot reference later locals. *)
   150	(*    Policy metadata and reserve action/closure names resolve after all actions parse. *)
   151	(* 3. Bare identifier in an expression names a prior local only. *)
   152	(* 4. lifetime is a positive UInt128 genesis allowance. It never resets. *)
   153	(* 5. horizon is a UInt128 UTC epoch-second exclusive upper bound at genesis. *)
   154	(* 6. floor_div(n,d) is the only division form and elaborates to FloorDiv(n,d). *)
   155	(* 7. Amount without a parameter is emit-only and records the operand unit. *)
   156	(* 8. Bool literals type as Bool. Bool is expression-only in this profile. *)
   157	(* 9. Source comments are forbidden so numeric-looking text cannot hide. *)
   158	(* 10. not binds tighter than comparison: `not a == b` parses `(not a) == b`. *)
   159	(* 11. Each action may write a state field at most once. Effect ordinals are *)
   160	(*     zero-based in source emit order within that action. Policy targets must exist. *)

/bin/bash -lc 'nl -ba experiments/moriarty-language/spec/semantics.md' in /home/charl/Moriarty-wt-moriarty-mc01-20260907-plan-profile

     1	# Moriarty bounded atomic profile: static and dynamic semantics
     2	
     3	Status: S2 provisional corrected candidate, specified-only and not frozen or implemented.
     4	Fresh independent Fable and GPT-6 audits of these exact bytes remain required.
     5	Profile `moriarty-bounded-atomic/1`; Core `moriarty-core/1`. The closed wire
     6	records and hash preimages in `typed-schemas.md`, simultaneous limits in
     7	`bounds.json`, and numeric rules in `numeric-profile.json` are normative with
     8	this document. A conflict is an error in the candidate, not a precedence choice.
     9	
    10	## Domains, source, and judgments
    11	
    12	Let `U = {0..2^128-1}`. A declared nominal unit has a canonical unit vector with
    13	one exponent `1`; UInt128 has the empty vector. Bool and Text have exactly `[]`
    14	as nonnumeric unit metadata; their explicit type distinguishes them from UInt128. A vector has at most eight
    15	nonzero components, each in `[-16,16]`. Stored types are UInt128, Text, and
    16	Amount<X>. Bool and Quantity<q> are expression/local types only. In particular,
    17	a `let` may contain Bool or a nontrivial product/quotient Quantity, and a local
    18	is not a stored result. Bool and Quantity cannot be constants, arguments,
    19	observations, state, `set` results, effect operands, obligations, or receipts.
    20	
    21	```text
    22	G |- e : T ! q                  expression and canonical unit vector
    23	G ; S ; L |- statement => S'   typed straight-line statement
    24	P |- source => SourceAST        complete parse, without recovery execution
    25	SourceAST |- check => TypedProgram
    26	TypedProgram |- lower => BoundProgram
    27	M ; genesis ; state ; request |- step => Rejected | Complete
    28	```
    29	
    30	There are no user functions, recursion, loops, dynamic evaluation, collections,
    31	or implicit iteration. Recursive-looking expression records are finite bounded
    32	trees, not executable recursion. Value and type names resolve declaration-before-use; a bare
    33	identifier resolves only to an earlier local. Duplicate names reject within each
    34	namespace. The separate namespaces are units, constants, state fields,
    35	observations, settlement bindings, field policies, effect kinds, actions, and
    36	(each separately per action) parameters and locals. Cross-namespace name
    37	collisions are permitted; prefixed references select the namespace.
    38	Policy target actions, rounding locals, and reserve action/closure names are forward
    39	metadata references: resolve them over the complete action table in stage 6,
    40	then require all target/value-local constraints before evaluation. A policy may
    41	precede or follow the action it covers. Its unit reference still requires a
    42	previous unit declaration. Settlement bindings may precede or follow the actions
    43	that use them, but their quantum unit must precede the settlement declaration.
    44	Status-rule state references require previously declared state fields;
    45	ReserveDecl and action-target/rounding metadata are position-independent.
    46	Effect schemas still precede their emits. Each action has exactly one `actor: Text` parameter. A word that
    47	exactly equals a grammar keyword is a keyword, never an identifier; only `asset`
    48	and `amount` are contextual effect-field labels. `not` binds tighter than a
    49	comparison, so `not a == b` parses `(not a) == b` and normally fails typing.
    50	
    51	Source spans are zero-based UTF-8 byte intervals `[start,end)`. Lowering retains
    52	the source hash, version, node IDs, types, unit vectors, resolution targets, and
    53	source references on Core statements and expressions. In moriarty-core/1 every
    54	sourceRef uses Source and exactly one span; other generated tags are reserved for
    55	MC02 and reject here. No compiler may discard or fabricate this binding. Exact
    56	token-boundary spans, structural parenthesis erasure and deterministic IDs are
    57	specified in `typed-schemas.md`; merely in-range spans are insufficient.
    58	
    59	## Arithmetic and evaluation order
    60	
    61	`uint(n)`, `text(s)`, `amount(n,X)`, `true`, and `false` have their explicit
    62	types. Numeric-looking Text is never coerced. `remaining` is UInt128 and reads
    63	the allowance before consumption by the current action.
    64	
    65	`+` and `-` require identical numeric types and vectors and retain that vector.
    66	`*` adds exponents. `floor_div(n,d)` subtracts denominator exponents and is the
    67	only division syntax. It evaluates numerator then denominator, rejects zero,
    68	and returns mathematical floor because all values are unsigned. Classification
    69	by the result vector produces UInt128, Amount<X>, or local-only Quantity<q>.
    70	
    71	Every literal, operand, and intermediate result must be in U. Multiplication and
    72	addition reject overflow; subtraction rejects underflow. The implementation
    73	must detect overflow before a later division, so `(MAX * 2)` rejects even if a
    74	later quotient would fit. No reassociation, cancellation, widening accepted
    75	semantics, floating point, hidden division, or implicit rounding is permitted.
    76	Host big integers may only detect rejection. `and` and `or` short-circuit left
    77	to right. Comparisons do not chain. Ordering requires equal numeric types and
    78	vectors. Equality requires the same type and, for numerics, vector.
    79	
    80	Statements execute against a private working copy in source order. Guards false
    81	reject. A let defines once. A set writes its declared state field and later reads
    82	see the new value. Each action may set a field at most once. Emit captures values
    83	at its position. Rejection exposes no working writes, effects, obligations, or
    84	authority consumption.
    85	
    86	## Static financial policy binding
    87	
    88	A policy target has one unambiguous identity:
    89	
    90	```text
    91	write(actionName,stateFieldName)
    92	effect(actionName,zeroBasedEmitOrdinal,effectFieldLabel)
    93	```
    94	
    95	Every Amount-valued `set` occurrence and every Amount-valued field occurrence
    96	in an `emit` has exactly one target in exactly one policy. No other occurrence
    97	may be targeted. A target must resolve to the named action, statement, declared
    98	field, and exact unit. Duplicate, missing, unknown, wrong-kind, wrong-unit, or
    99	out-of-range targets reject. Coverage is occurrence-based: copying, adding,
   100	zeroing, or emitting an Amount still requires an explicit target. Policies do
   101	not attach by similarly spelled names, and there is no default or heuristic
   102	propagation between targets.
   103	
   104	During an action, each computed numeric value also carries an internal finite
   105	set of FloorDiv node IDs. Literals, constants, action arguments, observations,
   106	`remaining`, and values read from the committed pre-state start with the empty
   107	set. Arithmetic unions operand sets, and a FloorDiv additionally inserts its own
   108	node ID. Lets retain the set; sets place it in the private working value for the
   109	rest of this action; reads and emits copy it. Provenance is checking metadata and
   110	is not persisted across actions.
   111	
   112	`rounding none` requires the target value's set to be empty. `rounding
   113	floor(a,l)` resolves statically to the unique let `l` in action `a`, whose root
   114	expression must be FloorDiv, and dynamically requires the target set to be the
   115	singleton containing that Core node. Multiple floor nodes at one target are
   116	unsupported and reject. This makes rounding propagation through a local, a
   117	same-action state write/read, or further exact arithmetic deterministic. A
   118	missing, ambiguous, future, non-FloorDiv, unreachable, or extra rounding node
   119	rejects without name guessing.
   120	
   121	`derivation`, `remainder`, `comparison`, and `proof` are documentary strings.
   122	Their exact bytes and the resolved target/node records are program-hash bound.
   123	The strings are not parsed, compared to source, or accepted as proof predicates;
   124	there is therefore no undefined “stale text” check. The proof string names a
   125	mandatory future claim only. Policy validation establishes binding and declared
   126	rounding provenance, not financial correctness, ACTUS conformance, or a proof.
   127	
   128	Both supplied examples explicitly cover every Amount set/effect occurrence.
   129	The swap's `output_calculated` provenance propagates through reserve/trader
   130	writes and the output transfer; its input and closure paths have no FloorDiv.
   131	The loan's accrued interest provenance propagates through `interest_due` and its
   132	DueCreated effect, while the later settlement begins with empty provenance from
   133	committed state and uses `rounding none`.
   134	
   135	## Effect schemas and settlement
   136	
   137	The fixed source schemas for Transfer, Fee, DueCreated, and DueSettled are exact
   138	in `typed-schemas.md`; arbitrary effect records are not permitted. Emit fields
   139	must occur exactly once in declaration order with exact types. Transfer and Fee
   140	propose asset movements. Due effects update the semantic obligation ledger.
   141	Neither proves actual movement, balance, custody, or ledger acceptance.
   142	
   143	A settlement declaration creates a one-to-one nominal-unit/ledger-asset binding.
   144	Its `asset` Text must be nonempty and its quantum must be a positive Amount of
   145	the declared unit. Across a manifest, binding names, units, and asset text values
   146	are each unique. Thus runtime selection is exact, never first-match: an emitted
   147	asset and Amount unit must resolve to the same unique binding or evaluation
   148	rejects `SETTLEMENT_BINDING_MISSING`; multiple candidates reject statically as
   149	`SETTLEMENT_BINDING_AMBIGUOUS`.
   150	
   151	Quantum orientation is exact: `quantum.value` nominal subunits equal one ledger
   152	base unit. For nominal `value`, require `value mod quantum.value = 0`, then
   153	`ledgerAmount = floor_div(value,quantum.value)`. Check all operands, quotient,
   154	and the reverse multiplication `ledgerAmount * quantum.value == value` in
   155	UInt128. A remainder rejects `SETTLEMENT_NON_DIVISIBLE`; zero quantum rejects
   156	statically; checked-conversion failure rejects `SETTLEMENT_OVERFLOW`. No rounding
   157	or dust disposal occurs. For quantum 10, nominal 20 converts to ledger 2; nominal
   158	15 rejects rather than converting to 1 or 2.
   159	
   160	Every Transfer and Fee requires that exact resolution. DueCreated requires its
   161	denomination Text equal its Amount unit identifier exactly. DueSettled requires
   162	that equality and its asset/unit settlement resolution. The Complete effect
   163	record includes the closed `SettlementResolution`, amount unit, quantum, and
   164	ledger amount; there is no unhashed side map or implicit unit erasure.
   165	
   166	For each `(asset,from,to,unit)` group containing DueSettled effects, the checked
   167	sum of their nominal amounts must equal the checked sum of Transfer amounts for
   168	the same asset with `from=debtor` and `to=creditor`, grouped by those parties.
   169	The corresponding checked ledger sums must also be equal. This permits the loan
   170	to combine PR and IP into one Transfer but prevents an obligation record from
   171	asserting settlement without matching movement intent. Unrelated swap Transfers
   172	need no DueSettled record. Fees are not settlement credit and remain included in
   173	gross-debit and net-goal authority checks.
   174	
   175	Asset authentication and actual ledger quantization remain external predicates.
   176	A wrapper must verify that binding text denotes the intended ledger asset and
   177	that the finalized movement equals the resolution. This profile only specifies
   178	the value and interface that must be checked.
   179	
   180	## Obligation transition and status derivation
   181	
   182	The state retains at most 128 `ObligationRecord` values, including settled
   183	identity tombstones. Identity is `(instanceId,dueId)` for the entire nonresetting
   184	genesis lifetime. Effects are processed in source emit order against a private
   185	obligation copy after their typed values and settlements are resolved.
   186	The delta arrays capture immutable snapshots at each successful event: DueCreated
   187	appends an Outstanding copy to created; DueSettled appends the updated Settled
   188	copy to settled. Creating, transferring and settling one due in the same action
   189	therefore retains both snapshots and a final Settled tombstone. It never rewrites
   190	the created snapshot to the final status.
   191	
   192	For DueCreated:
   193	
   194	1. amount must be positive; denomination must equal the Amount unit identifier;
   195	2. neither Outstanding nor Settled record may already have the due ID;
   196	3. capacity for one retained record must remain; and
   197	4. insert the exact debtor, creditor, denomination, unit, amount, and Outstanding.
   198	
   199	Zero amount rejects `OBLIGATION_ZERO`; denomination/unit disagreement rejects
   200	`OBLIGATION_MISMATCH`; duplicate identity rejects `OBLIGATION_DUPLICATE`; capacity
   201	failure rejects `OBLIGATION_CAPACITY`. For DueSettled, exactly one existing due ID must exist.
   202	Debtor, creditor, denomination, and unit must all equal it. Zero rejects
   203	`OBLIGATION_ZERO`. An
   204	amount below the outstanding amount rejects `OBLIGATION_PARTIAL_UNSUPPORTED`;
   205	an amount above rejects `OBLIGATION_EXCESS`; equality changes status to Settled
   206	without deleting the record. Unknown IDs reject `OBLIGATION_UNKNOWN`; an already
   207	Settled record rejects `OBLIGATION_ALREADY_SETTLED`; identity-field mismatch
   208	rejects `OBLIGATION_MISMATCH`. This first atomic profile neither partially settles
   209	nor recreates an ID. A later version may add a remaining-amount field and partial
   210	rules, but cannot reinterpret this profile.
   211	
   212	There is exactly one episode status rule and one agreement status rule. Before
   213	evaluation, the supplied status fields must equal deterministic derivation from
   214	the supplied values/obligations or reject `STATUS_MISMATCH`. After all provisional
   215	writes and obligation effects, derive again:
   216	
   217	- EpisodeStatus is Closed iff the declared state field equals the declared
   218	  stored literal under exact typed equality; otherwise Open.
   219	- RemainingNotional is the Amount in the declared state field, or the explicit
   220	  NotApplicable variant for `no_remaining_notional`.
   221	- AgreementStatus is Outstanding iff any obligation is Outstanding or a declared
   222	  remaining notional is greater than zero; otherwise NoOutstanding.
   223	
   224	Episode Closed therefore never implies Agreement NoOutstanding. In the loan,
   225	accrue creates separate `lam01:period1:PR` (500,000,000) and `...:IP`
   226	(33,972,602) Outstanding records and leaves notional 4,500,000,000 micro-USD.
   227	Settle marks both records Settled, closes the episode, and still derives agreement
   228	Outstanding with remaining notional 4,500,000,000. The tombstones prevent a
   229	duplicate create or settle. In the swap, remaining notional is NotApplicable;
   230	agreement obligation status is independent of whether the epoch is Open/Closed.
   231	
   232	## Genesis and lifecycle
   233	
   234	Genesis binds the program reference, bounds, initial state, positive lifetime,
   235	exclusive horizon, instance ID, principal/actor bindings, observation-provider
   236	bindings, and mandatory claim root. The initial state has revision 0, remaining
   237	equal to lifetime, no obligations, and deterministically derived statuses. A
   238	continuation, redeployment, new request, or receipt cannot replace genesis or
   239	increase remaining/horizon. Split, join, Pending, and continuation export are
   240	unsupported and cannot reset either bound.
   241	
   242	The genesis `requiredClaimRoot` must equal the domain-separated claimRoot of the
   243	manifest's exact ordered ClaimRequirement array. Post-trace
   244	ProofAcceptanceInput.claimEvidence supplies one matching ClaimEvidenceRef per
   245	requirement and no extras. The signed authority
   246	contains only the exact ClaimRequirement list and its root, not that evidence. The array must cover
   247	ContractProperty (including every policy proof identifier), IntentRefinement,
   248	TransitionValidity, and PredecessorHistory. External proof verification must bind
   249	each proof/public-input digest to the program, genesis/instance, predecessor set,
   250	authority, observations, before state, exact actionHash, action trace, and after state
   251	through the exact ProofContext and its committed objects.
   252	This source profile fixes the acceptance interface and fail-closed requirement;
   253	it supplies no PCD construction or verifier.
   254	
   255	Every input state must satisfy checked `revision + remaining == genesis.lifetime`.
   256	Every Complete action decrements remaining exactly once and increments revision
   257	exactly once. Remaining zero rejects before execution; revision overflow rejects.
   258	The horizon is the genesis UInt128 exclusive UTC epoch-second bound. `obs.now`
   259	is explicit and externally authenticated; the evaluator never reads a host
   260	clock. It requires `now < horizon`. The horizon guards repeated in the examples
   261	are intentional redundant developer diagnostics; this mandatory check applies
   262	even if the source guard is absent or drifts.
   263	
   264	Closure reserve is explicit, decidable, and hash-bound. Source
   265	`reserve swap for close;` lowers to `ReserveRule {action:"swap",closure:"close"}`.
   266	Stage 6 checks every reserve declaration as follows, rejecting `RESERVE_RULE`:
   267	
   268	1. Both names resolve to existing, distinct actions in the complete action table.
   269	2. Each reserved action occurs in at most one ReserveRule. A named closure cannot
   270	   itself occur as any ReserveRule.action. Multiple reserved actions may share
   271	   one closure.
   272	3. The reserved action contains a top-level Guard whose condition is exactly
   273	   `Gt(Remaining, Literal(UIntLiteral token "1"))` after structural parenthesis
   274	   erasure. Spans and the guard message do not affect this shape test. Equivalent
   275	   arithmetic or Boolean reformulations do not satisfy it. Other guards remain
   276	   permitted, and no instruction-position inference is used.
   277	
   278	No rule is inferred from an action name, emitted resources, repeatability, or
   279	example identity. A program without reserve declarations incurs no reserve check;
   280	the loan has none and receives no implicit exception. The swap explicitly declares
   281	the rule. It blocks that action when remaining is one, preserving the allowance
   282	for the named closure. It proves neither closure feasibility nor that the closure
   283	returns resources, and cannot reset lifetime or horizon. Keys, guards, balances,
   284	mandatory proofs and aggregate result limits can still prevent closure.
   285	
   286	## Binding equalities
   287	
   288	Stage 9 uses canonical byte equality for records/arrays and exact scalar equality.
   289	The independently supplied BoundProgram is rechecked and its ProgramRef recomputed.
   290	These predicates are mandatory; the code in parentheses rejects a mismatch:
   291	
   292	- input.program equals that recomputed ProgramRef; manifest.bounds equals
   293	  input.program.bounds and the actual bounds registry reference (`PROGRAM_BINDING`).
   294	- genesis.body.program equals input.program (`GENESIS_PROGRAM_BINDING`).
   295	- genesis.body.initialState equals manifest.initialState, preserving initial
   296	  literals and declaration order (`GENESIS_INITIAL_STATE_BINDING`).
   297	- genesis.body.lifetime equals manifest.lifetime (`GENESIS_LIFETIME_BINDING`).
   298	- genesis.body.horizon equals manifest.horizon (`GENESIS_HORIZON_BINDING`).
   299	- genesis.body.bounds equals input.program.bounds (`GENESIS_BOUNDS_BINDING`).
   300	- state.body.programHash equals input.program.programHash (`STATE_PROGRAM_BINDING`).
   301	- state.body.genesisHash equals genesis.genesisHash (`STATE_GENESIS_BINDING`).
   302	- state.body.instanceId equals genesis.body.instanceId (`INSTANCE_BINDING`).
   303	- authority.statement.program equals input.program (`AUTHORITY_PROGRAM_BINDING`);
   304	  its domain, genesisHash, instanceId and beforeStateHash equal genesis.body.domain,
   305	  genesis.genesisHash, genesis.body.instanceId and state.stateHash respectively
   306	  (`AUTHORITY_CONTEXT_BINDING`).
   307	- authority.statement.requiredClaims equals manifest.requiredClaims, and both its
   308	  requiredClaimRoot and genesis.body.requiredClaimRoot equal the recomputed
   309	  manifest claimRoot (`CLAIM_ROOT_BINDING`).
   310	- authority.statement.predecessors equals exactly `[state.stateHash]`
   311	  (`PREDECESSOR_BINDING`).
   312	- In ExactPlan mode, authority.statement.action equals input.action
   313	  (`EXACT_ACTION_BINDING`). In IntentRefinement mode, input.action.name occurs in
   314	  allowedActions (`INTENT_ACTION_FORBIDDEN`); every allowed name is declared.
   315	
   316	All supplied digest wrappers are independently recomputed (`DIGEST_MISMATCH`).
   317	Action name, argument names/order/types, state values/schema and observation
   318	names/order/types must match the manifest (`INPUT_SCHEMA`). State obligations
   319	have unique due IDs, positive correctly denominated amounts and declared units
   320	(`INPUT_SCHEMA`). At revision zero, state values equal manifest.initialState,
   321	remaining equals lifetime, obligations are empty and statuses are derived exactly
   322	(`GENESIS_STATE_BINDING`); later states require authenticated currentness/history.
   323	Stage 10 also checks checked revision + remaining == genesis lifetime, the
   324	exclusive horizon, valid interval, all prechecks and the principal-to-actor rule.
   325	
   326	## Authority, observations, and external assumptions
   327	
   328	Evaluation receives the exact closed `EvaluationInput` in `typed-schemas.md`.
   329	The compact ProgramRef must equal the recomputed full program reference and state/
   330	genesis bindings. The authenticated principal must equal the signed principal;
   331	exactly one genesis PrincipalBinding must map it to an actor; and that actor must
   332	equal the action's required `actor` Text argument. Thus source guards such as
   333	`arg.actor == const.borrower` constrain a program role, while the mandatory
   334	wrapper binding proves which authenticated principal supplied that actor. Text
   335	equality alone is never authentication.
   336	
   337	Every declared observation appears exactly once, with the genesis-bound provider
   338	and correct stored type. `now` is mandatory. The wrapper authenticates observation
   339	evidence and oracle/provider identity. The language neither asserts oracle truth
   340	nor permits an undeclared host read.
   341	
   342	ExactPlan authority signs the exact instance, ActionCall, writes, and enriched
   343	effects. IntentRefinement signs the exact instance, allowed actions, recipient/call permissions,
   344	gross debit caps including Fee, minimum net credits after debits and fees,
   345	predecessors, claim requirements, validity, principal, nonce, domain, genesis
   346	hash, input-state hash, and ProgramRef. Proof evidence is not signed; its exact
   347	ProofContext and the acyclic acceptance sequence are in typed-schemas.md. Checks use
   348	ledger amounts after exact quantum conversion. Refunds do not reduce gross debit.
   349	Only the principal-bound actor A may appear in any cap or goal; other actors
   350	reject `INTENT_ACTOR_SCOPE`. Every Transfer or Fee with from == A requires a cap
   351	for (A,asset), even for zero amount, or rejects `INTENT_DEBIT_UNCAPPED`. Checked
   352	sums of those ledger amounts must not exceed the corresponding caps
   353	(`INTENT_DEBIT_CAP`). Refunds never reduce these gross sums. For each goal,
   354	credits sum Transfer/Fee with to == A and debits sum Transfer/Fee with from == A
   355	for that asset. A self-transfer contributes to both sums. DueCreated and
   356	DueSettled contribute to neither sum, avoiding double-counting settlement records.
   357	A missing debit or credit sum is zero. The goal passes exactly when
   358	`checked(credits) >= checked(debits + minimumLedgerAmount)`; failure rejects
   359	`INTENT_NET_GOAL`. Every sum and addition is UInt128 in ledger units after quantum
   360	conversion; overflow rejects `INTENT_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW`. All Transfer/Fee
   361	recipients, including nonprincipal transfers, must appear in permittedRecipients
   362	(`INTENT_RECIPIENT_FORBIDDEN`). This language has no external-call effect, so a
   363	nonempty permittedCalls list rejects `INTENT_CALL_UNSUPPORTED`.
   364	
   365	Signature verification, key control, nonce freshness across durable history,
   366	state unconsumed/currentness, predecessor ordering/finality, observation truth,
   367	required contract/intent/transition/history PCD claims bound to the derived
   368	ProofContext, asset authenticity,
   369	balance conservation, and ledger application are external mandatory predicates.
   370	All pre-execution checks and post-trace backend verdicts must be true in their
   371	respective phases; missing implementations fail closed. A hash-linked receipt, local simulation, documentary proof string, or
   372	MockProver result is not PCD. Historical `MORIARTY-SIGN-v1` and
   373	`MORIARTY-OUTCOME-SIGN-v1` bytes/domains remain unchanged and cannot be relabeled
   374	as the new authority variants.
   375	
   376	## Deterministic validation and transition
   377	
   378	Validation occurs in these stages; diagnostics within one stage sort by
   379	`(primarySpan.startByte,primarySpan.endByte,code)`:
   380	
   381	1. UTF-8/source byte validation.
   382	2. Longest-match lexing, exact keyword classification, identifiers, integer and
   383	   JSON-string tokens; comments and plain slash reject.
   384	3. Complete parse; recovery never creates executable AST.
   385	4. Closed declarations, exact effect schemas, uniqueness, counts and AST bounds.
   386	5. Declaration-before-use value/type resolution and deterministic node IDs/spans.
   387	   Policy metadata uses complete-action-table resolution in stage 6.
   388	6. Types, unit vectors, let locality, UInt bounds, policy target/node coverage,
   389	   settlement one-to-one bindings, status rules, actor parameter and explicit
   390	   ReserveRule validation.
   391	7. All simultaneous source, AST, typed-program and program-shape bounds.
   392	   Per-category maxima never waive aggregate limits.
   393	8. Deterministic lowering, source maps, canonical SemanticManifest and full
   394	   BoundProgram representability, bounds hash and program hash recomputation.
   395	9. Closed canonical evaluation/genesis/state/action/authority/observation schemas,
   396	   all digest recomputations, and every listed Binding equality.
   397	10. Current genesis/state/revision/remaining/horizon plus external authority,
   398	    principal/actor, nonce, validity, observation, and predecessor checks.
   399	    EvaluationInput carries prechecks only; no claim evidence exists in this phase.
   400	11. Sequential guard/let/set/emit execution with checked arithmetic and policy
   401	    provenance in a private working copy.
   402	12. Resolve settlement, process obligations and transfer conservation, derive
   403	    candidate statuses/values/revision+1/remaining-1, and check exact-plan or
   404	    outcome refinement, gross/net, resources and full result representability.
   405	13. Finalize candidate stateHash, recomputed actionHash, Complete traceHash and
   406	    ProofContext without commit. Receive the separate ProofAcceptanceInput,
   407	    resolve bounded proof bytes and verify all mandatory claims against that exact
   408	    context. Accept only the matching trusted ProofAcceptanceVerdict and recheck
   409	    durable currentness/nonce atomically with commit. Any failure returns Rejected.
   410	    The numbered two-phase protocol and implementing API are in typed-schemas.md.
   411	
   412	Complete means the selected atomic action and signed/refined outcome completed.
   413	It does not mean the full contract is discharged. Pending is not a result variant;
   414	a request needing residual progress rejects `UNSUPPORTED_PENDING` before commit.
   415	
   416	## Closed diagnostics
   417	
   418	This is the closed DiagnosticCode registry. No other code is legal in this profile.
   419	Each code has exactly the stage below; a condition described elsewhere maps to
   420	this table even where its prose does not repeat the code. Malformed data stops
   421	before dependent semantic checks. Validate stages in numeric order and stop at
   422	the first failing stage; for execution-dependent stages, stop at the first failing
   423	operation in the specified evaluation/effect order. Where independent errors at
   424	that point coexist, choose the smallest `(primarySpan.startByte,primarySpan.endByte,code)`.
   425	Return exactly one Diagnostic (therefore the rejection itself remains bounded).
   426	Use the failing source expression/statement/declaration span when available, or
   427	`{startByte:"0",endByte:"0"}` for input/backend/global errors without a source node.
   428	`relatedSpans` is `[]`; `message` equals the code, except GUARD_FAILED uses the
   429	exact source guard message. Parsing/checking returns this same diagnostic directly.
   430	
   431	| Code | Stage | Rejection condition |
   432	|---|---:|---|
   433	| SOURCE_ENCODING | 1 | Invalid UTF-8 or source byte cap exceeded. |
   434	| LEXICAL_TOKEN | 2 | Invalid token, forbidden source comment/slash, noncanonical decimal token, invalid JSON string, surrogate, identifier or language-text lexical limit, forbidden/reserved identifier use. |
   435	| PARSE_ERROR | 3 | Source does not fully parse under grammar.ebnf, including missing or trailing tokens. |
   436	| DECLARATION_SCHEMA | 4 | Missing action, invalid header profile, missing/extra/malformed declaration or fixed effect schema/field order. |
   437	| DUPLICATE_NAME | 4 | Duplicate name in a declaration/parameter/local namespace, duplicate effect kind or field, or duplicate write to a field in an action. |
   438	| AST_BOUNDS | 4 | SourceAST exceeds any encoding bound, including declarations > 128. |
   439	| NAME_RESOLUTION | 5 | Unknown or premature value/type/unit reference or a bare identifier not resolving to a prior local. |
   440	| TYPE_MISMATCH | 6 | Operator, literal, initializer, set, argument declaration, effect field, stored/local type, or Bool/Text unit metadata violates exact typing. |
   441	| UINT_RANGE | 6 | Source integer outside UInt128, or lifetime is zero. |
   442	| UNIT_VECTOR | 6 | Invalid numeric unit vector/classification, component count or exponent bound. |
   443	| POLICY_TARGET | 6 | Duplicate, absent, unknown, wrong-kind, wrong-unit, out-of-range or nonfinancial policy target; uncovered Amount occurrence. |
   444	| POLICY_ROUNDING | 6 | Rounding reference is unknown, wrong-action, ambiguous, unknown, wrong-action, ambiguous, non-let or non-FloorDiv-root reference, or its root is not FloorDiv. |
   445	| SETTLEMENT_DECLARATION | 6 | Empty asset, nonpositive quantum, quantum/declared-unit mismatch or malformed settlement declaration. |
   446	| SETTLEMENT_BINDING_AMBIGUOUS | 6 | Duplicate settlement name, unit or asset prevents one-to-one binding. |
   447	| STATUS_RULE | 6 | Missing/duplicate status rule or rule field/literal has wrong type or missing declaration. |
   448	| ACTOR_PARAMETER | 6 | Action lacks exactly one actor:Text parameter. |
   449	| OBSERVATION_SCHEMA | 6 | Source lacks now:UInt128 or declares now at another type. |
   450	| RESERVE_RULE | 6 | Reserve declaration violates any of the three explicit reserve checks. |
   451	| PROGRAM_BOUNDS | 7 | Any simultaneous typed-program or program-shape bound fails; includes expression/instruction/count limits. |
   452	| SOURCE_MAP | 8 | AST span, typed annotation, node ID, resolution, Core tree, generatedTag or sourceRef differs from deterministic source lowering. |
   453	| PROGRAM_ENCODING | 8 | BoundProgram/manifest is noncanonical, malformed, out of aggregate bounds, or differs from deterministic lowering/hash. |
   454	| INPUT_SCHEMA | 9 | Noncanonical or malformed evaluation/genesis/state/action/authority/observation input, unknown fields, wrong argument/value schema/order, unsupported authority mode, invalid ordered/unique arrays or observation/principal binding schema. |
   455	| INPUT_BOUNDS | 9 | Any signing/evaluation encoding or input record/count bound fails. |
   456	| DIGEST_MISMATCH | 9 | Supplied program/source/bounds/genesis/state or other digest fails its registered recomputation. |
   457	| PROGRAM_BINDING | 9 | Input ProgramRef or manifest bounds differs from recomputed program/registry. |
   458	| GENESIS_PROGRAM_BINDING | 9 | Genesis ProgramRef differs from input.program. |
   459	| GENESIS_INITIAL_STATE_BINDING | 9 | Genesis initialState differs from manifest.initialState. |
   460	| GENESIS_LIFETIME_BINDING | 9 | Genesis lifetime differs from manifest.lifetime. |
   461	| GENESIS_HORIZON_BINDING | 9 | Genesis horizon differs from manifest.horizon. |
   462	| GENESIS_BOUNDS_BINDING | 9 | Genesis bounds differs from program.bounds. |
   463	| STATE_PROGRAM_BINDING | 9 | State programHash differs from program.programHash. |
   464	| STATE_GENESIS_BINDING | 9 | State genesisHash differs from genesis.genesisHash. |
   465	| INSTANCE_BINDING | 9 | State instanceId differs from genesis instanceId. |
   466	| AUTHORITY_PROGRAM_BINDING | 9 | Signed ProgramRef differs from input.program. |
   467	| AUTHORITY_CONTEXT_BINDING | 9 | Signed domain/genesis/instance/before-state binding differs from actual input. |
   468	| CLAIM_ROOT_BINDING | 9 | Signed requirements or signed/genesis claim root differs from generated manifest claims/root. |
   469	| PREDECESSOR_BINDING | 9 | Signed predecessor array is not exactly the singleton current before-state hash. |
   470	| EXACT_ACTION_BINDING | 9 | Signed ExactPlan ActionCall differs from EvaluationInput.action. |
   471	| INTENT_ACTION_FORBIDDEN | 9 | Outcome allowedActions contains an undeclared action or omits the selected action. |
   472	| GENESIS_STATE_BINDING | 9 | Revision-zero state differs from the prescribed genesis values, allowance, empty obligations or derived status. |
   473	| GENESIS_UNAUTHENTICATED | 10 | Trusted genesisValid is false or unavailable. |
   474	| SIGNATURE_INVALID | 10 | Trusted signatureValid is false/unavailable, including unsupported external algorithm. |
   475	| NONCE_STALE | 10 | Trusted pre-execution nonceFresh is false/unavailable. |
   476	| STATE_NOT_CURRENT | 10 | Trusted pre-execution stateCurrentAndUnconsumed is false/unavailable. |
   477	| OBSERVATION_UNAUTHENTICATED | 10 | Trusted observationsAuthentic is false/unavailable or supplied provider differs from genesis binding. |
   478	| PREDECESSOR_UNAUTHENTICATED | 10 | Trusted predecessorSetValid is false/unavailable. |
   479	| PRINCIPAL_BINDING | 10 | Authenticated principal differs from signed principal, has no unique genesis actor, or that actor differs from arg.actor. |
   480	| LIFETIME_INVARIANT | 10 | Checked revision + remaining overflows or differs from genesis.lifetime. |
   481	| LIFETIME_EXHAUSTED | 10 | remaining == 0. |
   482	| HORIZON_EXPIRED | 10 | Authenticated now >= genesis.horizon. |
   483	| VALIDITY_INTERVAL | 10 | notBefore <= now < notAfterExclusive <= genesis.horizon fails. |
   484	| STATUS_MISMATCH | 10 | Supplied non-genesis status/notional fields differ from derivation from values and obligations. |
   485	| ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW | 11 | Checked expression add/multiply or another numeric expression result exceeds UInt128. |
   486	| ARITHMETIC_UNDERFLOW | 11 | Expression subtraction would be negative. |
   487	| DIVISION_BY_ZERO | 11 | FloorDiv denominator equals zero. |
   488	| GUARD_FAILED | 11 | Evaluated guard condition is false. |
   489	| POLICY_PROVENANCE | 11 | An Amount write/emit value has nonempty provenance for rounding none or other than the exact singleton for rounding floor. |
   490	| SETTLEMENT_BINDING_MISSING | 12 | Transfer/Fee/DueSettled asset and amount unit do not resolve to the same unique binding. |
   491	| SETTLEMENT_NON_DIVISIBLE | 12 | Nominal amount is not exactly divisible by quantum. |
   492	| SETTLEMENT_OVERFLOW | 12 | Checked settlement conversion or inverse multiplication fails UInt128. |
   493	| OBLIGATION_ZERO | 12 | DueCreated or DueSettled amount is zero. |
   494	| OBLIGATION_DUPLICATE | 12 | DueCreated reuses any retained due ID, including a settled tombstone. |
   495	| OBLIGATION_CAPACITY | 12 | DueCreated would exceed retained obligation capacity. |
   496	| OBLIGATION_UNKNOWN | 12 | DueSettled has no matching retained due ID. |
   497	| OBLIGATION_ALREADY_SETTLED | 12 | DueSettled names an already settled record. |
   498	| OBLIGATION_MISMATCH | 12 | Due denomination differs from amount unit, or settlement debtor/creditor/denomination/unit differs from retained identity. |
   499	| OBLIGATION_PARTIAL_UNSUPPORTED | 12 | Positive settlement amount is below outstanding amount. |
   500	| OBLIGATION_EXCESS | 12 | Settlement amount exceeds outstanding amount. |
   501	| OBLIGATION_CONSERVATION | 12 | DueSettled nominal or ledger group sums differ from corresponding Transfer sums. |
   502	| OBLIGATION_SUM_OVERFLOW | 12 | Checked obligation/Transfer conservation group sum exceeds UInt128. |
   503	| EXACT_PLAN_MISMATCH | 12 | Produced write projection/effects differ in length, order or canonical value from signed exact arrays. |
   504	| INTENT_ACTOR_SCOPE | 12 | Any outcome cap or net goal names an actor other than the principal-bound actor. |
   505	| INTENT_DEBIT_UNCAPPED | 12 | Any principal outgoing Transfer/Fee lacks its actor/asset cap. |
   506	| INTENT_DEBIT_CAP | 12 | Checked principal gross ledger debits exceed a matching cap. |
   507	| INTENT_NET_GOAL | 12 | Checked credits < checked(debits + minimumLedgerAmount) for a goal. |
   508	| INTENT_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW | 12 | Any intent ledger sum or debits + minimum exceeds UInt128. |
   509	| INTENT_RECIPIENT_FORBIDDEN | 12 | Any Transfer/Fee recipient is absent from permittedRecipients. |
   510	| INTENT_CALL_UNSUPPORTED | 12 | Outcome permittedCalls is nonempty. |
   511	| REVISION_OVERFLOW | 12 | Incrementing revision by one exceeds UInt128. |
   512	| RESOURCE_BOUNDS | 12 | Executed instruction, expression, effect or other runtime count exceeds its bound. |
   513	| RESULT_BOUNDS | 12 | Candidate state or complete result wrapper violates its applicable encoding/size limit. |
   514	| UNSUPPORTED_PENDING | 12 | Requested result requires Pending, split/join, continuation export or residual progress in this atomic profile. |
   515	| PROOF_SCHEMA | 13 | ProofAcceptanceInput/Verdict is malformed, noncanonical or exceeds proofAcceptanceEncoding. |
   516	| PROOF_REQUIREMENTS | 13 | Evidence/proof arrays omit, duplicate, add, reorder or mismatch required claim kind/ID. |
   517	| PROOF_RESOLUTION | 13 | Resolver unavailable, empty or invalid handle, immutable resolution/byte-length mismatch or any resolved proof count/byte bound fails. |
   518	| PROOF_DIGEST | 13 | ClaimEvidenceRef.proofDigest differs from SHA256 of corresponding resolved proof bytes. |
   519	| PROOF_CONTEXT | 13 | Supplied context, actionHash, publicInputDigest or verdict context hash differs from the exact locally derived context. |
   520	| PROOF_INVALID | 13 | Trusted backend unavailable, unknown claim, verification failure, untrusted verdict or either verdict boolean false. |
   521	| COMMIT_CONFLICT | 13 | Candidate differs from local derivation, or durable state/nonce currentness changed before atomic consumption. |
   522	
   523	## Bounds, examples, and scope
   524	
   525	Every applicable bound in `bounds.json` holds simultaneously: source, AST, full
   526	BoundProgram, compact signing statement, state, and result each have their own
   527	byte/depth/node/record/array limits. The full program-hash preimage contains Core
   528	instructions and uses the manifest depth-40 limit. Expanded AST, typed-program
   529	and manifest records have separate finite byte/node limits from signed objects. A signing statement uses only
   530	ProgramRef and depth 16. Neither object embeds the other. Actual aggregate program
   531	nodes/bytes decide representability, so 64 actions each at their local maximum are
   532	not promised to fit the 32,768-node manifest. SourceAST.declarations has an
   533	explicit effective total cap of 128, including reserve declarations, even when
   534	all category limits fit. ResultEncoding applies to the entire Complete wrapper:
   535	a representable state may have no representable transition or closure because
   536	its result exceeds that cap. This is an accepted profile limitation; reject
   537	RESULT_BOUNDS without consumption. Neither finite syntax nor closure reserve
   538	provides a blanket transition or closure liveness guarantee.
   539	
   540	The loan example specifies one first LAM period, not ACTUS conformance. Its exact
   541	interest is 2,480,000,000/73 micro-USD, floor 33,972,602 with discarded remainder
   542	54/73; combined settlement is 533,972,602; remaining notional is 4,500,000,000.
   543	The swap specifies only the fixed input vector enforced by expected_output: exact
   544	output 1,994,000,000/100,997, floor 19,743, reserves 1,010,000 and 1,980,257. It
   545	is not a reusable constant-product implementation or Uniswap conformance.
   546	
   547	ACTUS calendar/event/convention/signed-value/quantity-price behavior and DeFi
   548	concentrated liquidity, hooks, partial fill, routing, convergence, governance,
   549	and the rest of all 32/72 target rows remain mandatory versioned extensions and
   550	MC07 work. No source example, schema check, arithmetic calculation, or future
   551	review establishes parser/compiler implementation, native proof, PCD, ledger
   552	acceptance, compiler correspondence, safety, or corpus conformance.
   553	
   554	Changing numeric width/order/rounding, unit or quantum meaning, codec, bounds,
   555	effect/obligation/status/authority schema, lifecycle, hashing, or lowering creates
   556	a new semantic profile and new program hashes/domains. It invalidates affected
   557	proofs, certificates, theorem instances, vectors, and audits, while historical
   558	artifacts remain bound to their original bytes and predicates.
   559	
   560	
   561	## Count scopes and parser implementation
   562	
   563	In bounds.json programShape, policyTargets is the total number of target entries
   564	across all field policies; effectFields applies separately to each effect schema.
   565	argumentFieldsPerEntrypoint, instructionsPerEntrypoint, localsPerEntrypoint,
   566	expressionNodesPerEntrypoint and effectsPerEntrypoint apply to each action.
   567	expressionDepthRootOne applies to each expression tree. All declaration-category
   568	counts and reserveRules are program totals. obligationRecordsIncludingSettled
   569	applies to each state; expressionUnitComponents and absoluteUnitExponent apply
   570	to each unit vector/component; predecessorFanIn applies to each transition.
   571	Parenthesis nesting has no separate limit: the finite source byte cap bounds it.
   572	A conforming parser must handle profile-valid redundant parentheses without an
   573	implementation-specific recursion-depth rejection.
   574	
   575	Outcome intent debit caps do not bound new nominal obligations. DueCreated and
   576	DueSettled are excluded from ledger debit/credit sums; allowedActions and mandatory
   577	contract/refinement proofs still govern those transitions. Applications requiring
   578	an explicit signed nominal-debt cap need a future profile extension.
   579	
   580	Freeze status is an external approval record bound to exact file digests. Do not
   581	edit status strings inside bounds.json to record approval; its exact bytes are
   582	part of boundsHash. Historical candidate status text remains its provenance.
